You can use arguments like `silent=True` to suppress errors or `force=True` to ignore the content type.\\n  - `request.json` is a shortcut that attempts to return the parsed JSON data directly (or None).\\n- Since Flask 2.2, a 415 status is returned if the content type isn’t `application/json` and `force=False`; malformed JSON with `silent=False` raises a 400, otherwise returns None【3:9†source】【3:4†source】.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Deprecated and Removed APIs (relevant to JSON and more)\\n\\n- **`json_encoder` and `json_decoder`**: These attributes on app/blueprint and the corresponding classes have been removed in Flask 3.0. You should now use a custom JSON provider by subclassing `flask.json.provider.DefaultJSONProvider`. If you previously set `app.json_encoder`, migrate your custom logic there【3:7†source】【3:8†source】.\\n- All previously deprecated code prior to 3.0 (including `__version__`, `json_encoder`, etc.) has been removed.\\n- The `before_first_request` decorators and `json.htmlsafe_dumps`/`htmlsafe_dump` are also removed.\\n- The `JSON_AS_ASCII`, `JSON_SORT_KEYS`, `JSONIFY_MIMETYPE`, and >= 2.2, Blinker >= 1.9 are required.\\n- Option added to control automatic OPTION responses.\\n- `open_resource()` and similar functions can now take an encoding parameter (default: utf-8).\\n- `Request.max_content_length` can now be customized per-request; new config options for form resource limits.\\n- Key rotation support for `SECRET_KEY_FALLBACKS`, request trusted host checking, and DefaultJSONProvider\\nfrom datetime import datetime\\n\\nclass CustomJSONProvider(DefaultJSONProvider):\\n    def default(self, obj):\\n        if isinstance(obj, datetime):\\n            return obj.isoformat()\\n        return super().default(obj)\\n\\napp = Flask(__name__)\\napp.json = CustomJSONProvider(app)\\n```\\nSee Flask’s new [JSON customization The `request.json` property simply delegates to `get_json()` under the hood, but may return `None` if the content mimetype isn’t JSON (`application/json`). You can always use `get_json(force=True)` to skip the mimetype check, but this is rare and discouraged.\\n\\n- **Behavioral Details**:  \\n  - `request.get_json()` parses the body as JSON if the mimetype is correct, otherwise returns `None` by default. If the data cannot be parsed, it will raise a Bad Request error (400).\\n  - If you want to handle malformed payloads manually, you can override `Request.on_json_loading_failed()` in your custom request class【3:7†source】.\\n\\n- **Deprecated APIs**:  \\n  - The `request.json` property simply delegates to `get_json()` under the hood, but may return `None` if the content mimetype isn’t JSON (`application/json`). You can always use `get_json(force=True)` to skip the mimetype check, but this is rare and discouraged.\\n\\n- **Behavioral Details**:  \\n  - `request.get_json()` parses the body as JSON if the mimetype is correct, otherwise returns `None` by default. If the data cannot be parsed, it will raise a Bad Request error (400).\\n  - If you want to handle malformed payloads manually, you can override `Request.on_json_loading_failed()` in your custom request class【3:7†source】.\\n\\n- **Deprecated APIs**:  \\n  - In 3.1.x, no deprecation is specifically mentioned for `request.get_json()` or `request.json` in the official changelog or upgrade notes.\\n  - Some older Flask utilities and properties are deprecated and/or removed, but these primarily relate to internal context handling and not to JSON request processing【3:1†source】【3:6†source】.\\n\\n### Changelog Key Points for 3.1.0\\n\\n- **Minimum Python requirement increased**: Python 3.8 support dropped.\\n- **Dependency bumps**: Minimum versions of Werkzeug >= 3.1, ItsDangerous >= 2.2, and Blinker >= 1.9.\\n- **No JSON API deprecations listed**: The most significant recent removals/deprecations relate to context management and signature changes, not JSON handling.\\n\\n### JSON Handling Best Practices (Current in 3.1.x):\\n- **Always use `request.get_json()`** for clarity and explicit error handling.\\n- Use the `silent=True` argument if you want to gracefully handle bad or missing JSON (returns `None` instead of error).\\n- If you require JSON, ensure the client sets `Content-Type: application/json`.\\n\\n**References and further details are available in the [Flask 3.1.x changelog](https://flask.palletsprojects.com/en/3.1.x/changes/), and on the [request.get_json API docs](https://flask.palletsprojects.com/en/3.1.x/api/#flask.Request.get_json)**【3:1†source】【3:5†source】【3:6†source】【3:7†source】.\\n\\n---\\n\\n**Summary:**  \\n- `request.get_json()` remains the standard for accessing JSON request data.\\n- No deprecation for these APIs in 3.1.0.\\n- Changelog for 3.1.0 mainly bumps minimum dependencies and removes deprecated internals, not JSON access.\\n- Always prefer `get_json()` over `request.json` for clarity. \\n\\nLet me know if you need details about specific deprecated APIs or internal changes!\",\"annotations\":[{\"text\":\"\",\"start_index\":1110,\"end_index\":1122,\"url_citation\":{\"title\":\"python 3.x - This is due to a breaking change introduced in Flask 3.x: the `request.json` property now requires the `Content-Type` header to be `application/json`, and if it's not, it will raise a `BadRequest` error instead of returning `None` (as was the case in older Flask versions).\\n\\n### Symptoms\\n\\n- Tests that previously worked now raise exceptions about missing or malformed JSON.\\n- The error often looks like:\\n  ```\\n  werkzeug.exceptions.BadRequest: 400 Bad Request: Did not attempt to load JSON data because the request Content-Type was not 'application/json'.\\n  ```\\n\\n### Why This Happens in Strawberry\\n\\nStrawberry’s Flask integration uses `request.json` inside `GraphQLView` to parse incoming payloads for GraphQL operations. If tests (or clients) send requests with a different `Content-Type` or omit the header, `request.json` will now fail in Flask 3.x.\\n\\n### How to Fix\\n\\nThere are two ways to address this:\\n\\n#### 1. Fix Your Tests/Input\\n\\n**Ensure every POST request to `/graphql` sets:**\\n```\\nContent-Type: application/json\\n```\\nThis aligns with Flask 3.x requirements.\\n\\nFor example, in pytest Flask client:\\n```python\\nresponse = client.post(\\n    \\\"/graphql\\\",\\n    json={...},  # or data=json.dumps({...}), but not both\\n    headers={\\\"Content-Type\\\": \\\"application/json\\\"}\\n)\\n```\\n\\n#### 2. Patch Strawberry’s GraphQLView\\n\\nIf you need compatibility with clients that *might* send the wrong header, subclass or patch the GraphQLView to use `request.get_json(silent=True)` instead of `request.json`. This is due to a breaking change introduced in Flask 3.x: the `request.json` property now requires the `Content-Type` header to be `application/json`, and if it's not, it will raise a `BadRequest` error instead of returning `None` (as was the case in older Flask versions).\\n\\n### Symptoms\\n\\n- Tests that previously worked now raise exceptions about missing or malformed JSON.\\n- The error often looks like:\\n  ```\\n  werkzeug.exceptions.BadRequest: 400 Bad Request: Did not attempt to load JSON data because the request Content-Type was not 'application/json'.\\n  ```\\n\\n### Why This Happens in Strawberry\\n\\nStrawberry’s Flask integration uses `request.json` inside `GraphQLView` to parse incoming payloads for GraphQL operations. If tests (or clients) send requests with a different `Content-Type` or omit the header, `request.json` will now fail in Flask 3.x.\\n\\n### How to Fix\\n\\nThere are two ways to address this:\\n\\n#### 1. Fix Your Tests/Input\\n\\n**Ensure every POST request to `/graphql` sets:**\\n```\\nContent-Type: application/json\\n```\\nThis aligns with Flask 3.x requirements.\\n\\nFor example, in pytest Flask client:\\n```python\\nresponse = client.post(\\n    \\\"/graphql\\\",\\n    json={...},  # or data=json.dumps({...}), but not both\\n    headers={\\\"Content-Type\\\": \\\"application/json\\\"}\\n)\\n```\\n\\n#### 2.
